<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the generalized Ramanujan conjecture for globally generic cuspidal automorphic representations of split connected adjoint exceptional groups over global function fields. Every local component is tempered, without restrictions on characteristic or ramification depth. The proof extends the unramified Ramanujan theorem of a companion paper to all ramified places.

- **claim-exceptional-global-temperedness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $G/F$ be split connected adjoint absolutely simple of type $G_2$, $F_4$, $E_6$, $E_7$, or $E_8$. If $\pi=\bigotimes'_v\pi_v$ is a complex globally generic cuspidal automorphic representation of $G(\mathbb A_F)$, then $\pi_v$ is tempered for every place $v$. There is no restriction on the characteristic of $F$, the ramification depth, or the local representation type.
  - Explanation: Global genericity and cuspidality force temperedness at every place for the listed split adjoint exceptional groups, including ramified places.
- **claim-compatible-whittaker-data** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-whittaker)): Every standard Levi subgroup $H$ of $G$ has split connected center. Every smooth character of its maximal unipotent subgroup is trivial on the nonsimple positive root groups. Its nondegenerate unitary characters form a single $T(k)$-orbit.
  - Explanation: Adjoint root coordinates give split connected Levi centers and allow compatible Whittaker characters, including in small characteristic.
- **claim-semisimple-parameter-compatibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-parameters)): For the split groups considered here the following hold.* 1. *An irreducible smooth local representation $\sigma$ has a semisimple Weil parameter $\rho_\sigma$, with finite inertia image. The parametrization is compatible with normalized parabolic induction, tori and local class field theory, group isomorphisms, central characters, and twists through algebraic characters of the group.* 2. *A cuspidal automorphic representation $\Pi$ over a function field, with finite-order central character, occurs in an excursion summand with a semisimple global parameter $\Sigma_\Pi$. This parameter is defined over a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_\ell$ and matches normalized Satake parameters away from a finite set.* 3. *At every place $v$, the semisimplification of $\Sigma_\Pi\|_{W_{F_v}}$ is conjugate to $\rho_{\Pi_v}$.
  - Explanation: The parameter inputs supply local semisimple Weil parameters, occurring global excursion parameters, and compatibility at every place. They do not identify monodromy operators.
- **claim-tempered-parameter-completion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-tempered-completion)): If $\sigma$ is tempered on a standard Levi subgroup $M$ of $G$, then $\rho_\sigma$ has a tempered completion $(\rho_\sigma,N_M)$ as in <a href="#eq:completion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:completion">[eq:completion]</a>. This holds in every positive characteristic.
  - Explanation: Tempered inducing representations admit completions with bounded Weil image in the associated tempered parameter. The Weil part of the associated Weil–Deligne pair can nevertheless be unbounded.
- **claim-global-restriction-weil-part** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-wd-semisimplification)): Let $\Sigma$ be a continuous $\ell$-adic representation of a local decomposition group into $\widehat H$, defined over a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_\ell$. If $(r,N)$ is its Frobenius-semisimplified Weil–Deligne pair, then $r$ is conjugate to the semisimplification of $\Sigma\|_{W_k}$.
  - Explanation: Removing monodromy exponentials and the unipotent part of Frobenius preserves semisimplification inside the dual group, leaving the Weil part of the Frobenius-semisimplified pair.
- **claim-monodromy-independent-factor-ratio** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-monodromy-ratio)): Let $r$ be a Frobenius-semisimple Weil representation on a finite-dimensional complex vector space $V$, with finite inertial image. If $N$ and $N'$ are nilpotent operators such that $(r,N)$ and $(r,N')$ are Weil–Deligne representations, then $$\frac{L(1-s,(r,N)^\vee)}{L(s,(r,N))} \doteq \frac{L(1-s,(r,N')^\vee)}{L(s,(r,N'))}.$$ More precisely, put $W=V^{r(I_k)}$, $K=\ker(N\|_W)$, and $F=r(\mathrm{Fr})$. If $R_N(s)$ denotes the ratio on the left, then $$\label{eq:monodromy-ratio-unit} \frac{R_0(s)}{R_N(s)} =\det\bigl(-Q^{-s}F\mid W/K\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Changing monodromy over a fixed Weil representation changes the functional-equation ratio only by a nonvanishing monomial. The determinant formula makes the omitted factor explicit.
- **claim-pure-factor-and-determinant** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pure-determinant)): Let $(r,N)$ be a finite-dimensional Frobenius-semisimple Weil–Deligne representation, pure of weight zero. Then $L(s,(r,N))$ is holomorphic and nonzero for $\operatorname{Re}(s)>0$, and $$\label{eq:pure-determinant} \left\|\det r(\mathrm{Fr})\right\|=1.$$ Every Weil–Deligne direct summand of $(r,N)$ is also pure of weight zero and satisfies these conclusions.
  - Explanation: Symmetry of the monodromy filtration forces the Frobenius determinant to have absolute value equal to unity. Purity also controls local-factor poles and passes to Weil–Deligne direct summands.
- **claim-regular-adjoint-open-orbit** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-open-orbit)): Let $\widehat G$ be a connected semisimple complex algebraic group, and let $r:W_k\to\widehat G$ be a Frobenius-semisimple Weil parameter with finite inertial image. Write $$\mathfrak e=\operatorname{Lie}(\widehat G)^{r(I_k)}, \qquad \mathfrak e_a= \ker\bigl(\operatorname{Ad}(r(\mathrm{Fr}))-a \mid\mathfrak e\bigr) \quad(a\in\mathbb C^\times),$$ and let $C=Z_{\widehat G}(r(W_k))$. If $(r,N)$ is a Weil–Deligne parameter and $L(s,\operatorname{Ad}\circ(r,N))$ is regular at $s=1$, then the orbit $C\cdot N$ is open in $\mathfrak e_{Q^{-1}}$. In particular, if $N_1,N_2$ are two monodromy operators over $r$ whose adjoint $L$-factors are regular at $1$, then some $c\in C$ satisfies $\operatorname{Ad}(c)N_1=N_2$.
  - Explanation: Adjoint regularity makes the orbit tangent map surjective through the Killing-form pairing. The resulting open orbit is unique, so regular completions over the same Weil parameter are conjugate.
- **claim-total-local-coefficient-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-comparison)): For $H,P,M$, and $\sigma$ as above, let $(\rho_\sigma,N_\sigma)$ be any Weil–Deligne pair in $\widehat M$ with Weil part the semisimple local parameter of $\sigma$. Then $$C_H(z,\sigma)\ \doteq\ \prod_b \frac{L\bigl(1-b(z),r_b^\vee\circ(\rho_\sigma,N_\sigma)\bigr)} {L\bigl(b(z),r_b\circ(\rho_\sigma,N_\sigma)\bigr)}. \label{eq:coefficient-comparison}$$ In particular, one may take $N_\sigma=0$. The equality is an identity of meromorphic functions of the unramified twists, up to a nonzero constant times a monomial in twist coordinates.
  - Explanation: The total local coefficient has the same divisor as the product of Weil–Deligne factor ratios attached to central-weight summands of the dual nilradical. This comparison permits arbitrary completions, including zero monodromy.
- **claim-whittaker-family-specialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-whittaker-family)): The twisted coinvariants of the induced family $I_P^H(\sigma_z)$ form a free rank-one $\mathcal A$-module, and this description commutes with specialization of $z$. The Jacquet-integral Whittaker functionals give a generator of its dual whose specialization is nonzero at every twist. Consequently, the inverse scalar induced by $A_H$ on these lines is $C_H(z,\sigma)$ up to a unit of $\mathcal A$.
  - Explanation: The Whittaker coinvariant line is free over the twist coordinate ring and specializes without losing its generator. This permits evaluation of the inverse intertwining scalar at reducibility points.
- **claim-elementary-weyl-factorization** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-elementary-moves)): Let $L$ be a standard Levi of $H$, and suppose $v\Delta_L\subseteq \Delta_H$. There is a length-additive factorization $$v=v_t\cdots v_1$$ with the following properties. Put $v_{<j}=v_{j-1}\cdots v_1$ and $L_j=v_{<j}Lv_{<j}^{-1}$. Then $L_j$ is standard, it is a maximal proper Levi in a standard Levi subgroup $H_j$, and $$\Delta_{H_j}=\Delta_{L_j}\cup\{\alpha_j\},\qquad v_j=w_{H_j}w_{L_j}.$$ Moreover, $$\operatorname{Inv}(v) =\bigsqcup_{j=1}^t v_{<j}^{-1}\operatorname{Inv}(v_j). \label{eq:inversion-partition}$$ The analogous partition holds for coroots.
  - Explanation: A Weyl element preserving the required simple-root condition factors into elementary maximal-parabolic moves. Length additivity partitions the inversion roots and coroots used in multiplicativity.
- **claim-generic-langlands-adjoint-regularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-generic-regularity)): Let $G$ be split adjoint absolutely simple over $k$. Suppose a generic irreducible representation $\tau$ is the Langlands quotient of $I_P^G(\sigma_\nu)$, where $P=MR$ is a proper standard parabolic, $\sigma$ is tempered, and $\nu$ is in the open positive chamber for $P$. Choose a tempered completion $(\rho_\sigma,N_M)$ from Theorem <a href="#thm:tempered-completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:tempered-completion">4</a>, twist it by $\nu$, and include it in $\widehat G$. The resulting pair $(r,N_M)$ has $r\simeq\rho_\tau$ and $$L(s,\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\circ(r,N_M))\quad\text{is regular at }s=1.$$
  - Explanation: Genericity makes the long intertwiner nonzero on Whittaker lines. The coefficient comparison then excludes poles in the opposite-nilradical factors and yields adjoint regularity for the twisted tempered completion.
- **claim-pure-completion-temperedness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-local-criterion)): Let $G$ be split adjoint absolutely simple over a local function field $k$, and let $\tau$ be an irreducible generic smooth representation of $G(k)$. Suppose $\rho_\tau$ admits a Weil–Deligne completion $(\rho_\tau,N_*)$ whose adjoint representation is pure of weight zero. Then $\tau$ is tempered.
  - Explanation: A pure adjoint completion forces a generic local representation to be tempered. Otherwise open-orbit conjugacy transfers purity to a positively twisted inducing completion, whose nilradical determinant contradicts purity.
- **claim-companion-unramified-temperedness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-unramified)): Let $F$ be the function field of a smooth projective geometrically connected curve over a finite field. Let $G/F$ be split connected adjoint absolutely simple. If a complex cuspidal automorphic representation of $G(\mathbb A_F)$ has a generic unramified component, then all its unramified components are tempered.
  - Explanation: A generic unramified component supplies temperedness at all unramified places. This cited companion result provides the global source of pointwise adjoint purity.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-parameters",
        "exact_quote": "For the split groups considered here the following hold.*\n\n1.  *An irreducible smooth local representation $\\sigma$ has a semisimple Weil parameter $\\rho_\\sigma$, with finite inertia image. The parametrization is compatible with normalized parabolic induction, tori and local class field theory, group isomorphisms, central characters, and twists through algebraic characters of the group.*\n\n2.  *A cuspidal automorphic representation $\\Pi$ over a function field, with finite-order central character, occurs in an excursion summand with a semisimple global parameter $\\Sigma_\\Pi$. This parameter is defined over a finite extension of $\\mathbb Q_\\ell$ and matches normalized Satake parameters away from a finite set.*\n\n3.  *At every place $v$, the semisimplification of $\\Sigma_\\Pi|_{W_{F_v}}$ is conjugate to $\\rho_{\\Pi_v}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.4"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-monodromy-ratio",
        "exact_quote": "Let $r$ be a Frobenius-semisimple Weil representation on a finite-dimensional complex vector space $V$, with finite inertial image. If $N$ and $N'$ are nilpotent operators such that $(r,N)$ and $(r,N')$ are Weil–Deligne representations, then $$\\frac{L(1-s,(r,N)^\\vee)}{L(s,(r,N))}\n \\doteq\n \\frac{L(1-s,(r,N')^\\vee)}{L(s,(r,N'))}.$$ More precisely, put $W=V^{r(I_k)}$, $K=\\ker(N|_W)$, and $F=r(\\mathrm{Fr})$. If $R_N(s)$ denotes the ratio on the left, then $$\\label{eq:monodromy-ratio-unit}\n \\frac{R_0(s)}{R_N(s)}\n   =\\det\\bigl(-Q^{-s}F\\mid W/K\\bigr).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-014.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-monodromy-ratio",

If $R_N(s)$ denotes the ratio on the left, then $$\\label{eq:monodromy-ratio-unit}\n \\frac{R_0(s)}{R_N(s)}\n   =\\det\\bigl(-Q^{-s}F\\mid W/K\\bigr).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-014.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $r$ be a Frobenius-semisimple Weil representation on a finite-dimensional complex vector space $V$, with finite inertial image. If $R_N(s)$ denotes the ratio on the left, then $$\\label{eq:monodromy-ratio-unit}\n \\frac{R_0(s)}{R_N(s)}\n   =\\det\\bigl(-Q^{-s}F\\mid W/K\\bigr).$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pure-determinant",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(r,N)$ be a finite-dimensional Frobenius-semisimple Weil–Deligne representation, pure of weight zero.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-open-orbit",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\widehat G$ be a connected semisimple complex algebraic group, and let $r:W_k\\to\\widehat G$ be a Frobenius-semisimple Weil parameter with finite inertial image. Write $$\\mathfrak e=\\operatorname{Lie}(\\widehat G)^{r(I_k)},\n \\qquad\n \\mathfrak e_a=\n \\ker\\bigl(\\operatorname{Ad}(r(\\mathrm{Fr}))-a\n                 \\mid\\mathfrak e\\bigr)\n \\quad(a\\in\\mathbb C^\\times),$$ and let $C=Z_{\\widehat G}(r(W_k))$. If $(r,N)$ is a Weil–Deligne parameter and $L(s,\\operatorname{Ad}\\circ(r,N))$ is regular at $s=1$, then the orbit $C\\cdot N$ is open in $\\mathfrak e_{Q^{-1}}$. In particular, if $N_1,N_2$ are two monodromy operators over $r$ whose adjoint $L$-factors are regular at $1$, then some $c\\in C$ satisfies $\\operatorname{Ad}(c)N_1=N_2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-open-orbit",

If $(r,N)$ is a Weil–Deligne parameter and $L(s,\\operatorname{Ad}\\circ(r,N))$ is regular at $s=1$, then the orbit $C\\cdot N$ is open in $\\mathfrak e_{Q^{-1}}$. In particular, if $N_1,N_2$ are two monodromy operators over $r$ whose adjoint $L$-factors are regular at $1$, then some $c\\in C$ satisfies $\\operatorname{Ad}(c)N_1=N_2$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-whittaker-family",
        "exact_quote": "The twisted coinvariants of the induced family $I_P^H(\\sigma_z)$ form a free rank-one $\\mathcal A$-module, and this description commutes with specialization of $z$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-elementary-moves",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a standard Levi of $H$, and suppose $v\\Delta_L\\subseteq\n\\Delta_H$. There is a length-additive factorization $$v=v_t\\cdots v_1$$ with the following properties. Put $v_{<j}=v_{j-1}\\cdots v_1$ and $L_j=v_{<j}Lv_{<j}^{-1}$. Then $L_j$ is standard, it is a maximal proper Levi in a standard Levi subgroup $H_j$, and $$\\Delta_{H_j}=\\Delta_{L_j}\\cup\\{\\alpha_j\\},\\qquad\n v_j=w_{H_j}w_{L_j}.$$ Moreover, $$\\operatorname{Inv}(v)\n   =\\bigsqcup_{j=1}^t v_{<j}^{-1}\\operatorname{Inv}(v_j).\n \\label{eq:inversion-partition}$$ The analogous partition holds for coroots.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-elementary-moves",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a standard Levi of $H$, and suppose $v\\Delta_L\\subseteq\n\\Delta_H$. Then $L_j$ is standard, it is a maximal proper Levi in a standard Levi subgroup $H_j$, and $$\\Delta_{H_j}=\\Delta_{L_j}\\cup\\{\\alpha_j\\},\\qquad\n v_j=w_{H_j}w_{L_j}.$$ Moreover, $$\\operatorname{Inv}(v)\n   =\\bigsqcup_{j=1}^t v_{<j}^{-1}\\operatorname{Inv}(v_j).\n \\label{eq:inversion-partition}$$ The analogous partition holds for coroots.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.4"

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Global genericity** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A cuspidal automorphic representation $\pi$ of $G(\mathbb A_F)$ is *globally generic* if some vector has a nonzero Whittaker coefficient $$\int_{N(F)\backslash N(\mathbb A_F)} f(ng)\psi(n)^{-1}\,dn$$ for a character $\psi$ nontrivial in every simple-root coordinate.
- **Temperedness** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Here temperedness means that the unitary local representation is weakly contained in the regular representation.
- **Whittaker character** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For a split group, a Whittaker character is a smooth unitary character of its maximal unipotent subgroup that is nontrivial on every simple root group.
- **Generic smooth representation** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A smooth representation is *generic* if it admits a nonzero equivariant functional for such a character.
- **Unramified twist** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For $z\in\mathfrak a_{M,\mathbb C}^*$, the notation $\sigma_z$ denotes the unramified twist in which $s\chi$ acts as $\|\chi\|^s$.
- **Frobenius-semisimple Weil–Deligne parameter** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A Frobenius-semisimple Weil–Deligne parameter for $H$ is a pair $(r,N)$, where $r:W_k\longrightarrow\widehat H(\mathbb C)$ has finite inertia image and semisimple Frobenius, and $N\in\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(\widehat H)$ is nilpotent with $$\label{eq:wd-relation} \mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(r(w))N=\|w\|N.$$
- **Local Weil–Deligne factor** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For an algebraic representation of $\widehat H$ on $V$, use the same symbols for the induced operators and set $$\label{eq:local-factor} L(s,V)=\det\left(1-Q^{-s}r(\mathop{\mathrm{Fr}})\mid(\ker N)^{r(I_k)}\right)^{-1}.$$
- **Tempered parameter and associated completion** ([source](#eq:completion)): A tempered $L$-parameter is a homomorphism $\phi:W_k\times\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb C)\longrightarrow\widehat H(\mathbb C)$, algebraic on $\mathrm{SL}_2$, whose Weil image is bounded.
- **Equality up to a monomial unit** ([source](#pm-h-7)): We write $f\doteq g$ for equality up to a nonzero constant times a monomial in the exponential unramified-twist coordinates.
- **Monodromy filtration centered at zero** ([source](#pm-h-12)): Recall that the *monodromy filtration centered at zero* of a nilpotent operator $N$ on $V$ is the unique finite increasing filtration $M_\bullet V$, indexed by the integers, satisfying $$N(M_jV)\subset M_{j-2}V, \qquad N^j:\operatorname{Gr}^{M}_jV \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } \operatorname{Gr}^{M}_{-j}V \quad (j\geq 0).$$
- **Purity of weight zero** ([source](#pm-h-12)): We say that $(r,N)$ is *pure of weight zero*, with respect to the fixed complex realization of the coefficients, if every Frobenius eigenvalue on $\operatorname{Gr}^{M}_jV$ has absolute value $Q^{j/2}$.
- **Inversion set** ([source](#pm-h-16)): For a Weyl element $v$, set $$\operatorname{Inv}(v) =\{\beta\in\Phi_H^+:v\beta\in-\Phi_H^+\}.$$

| q-positive-twist-strict-determinant-bound | Absolute value of twisted Frobenius determinant on the positive dual nilradical | < | $1$ | The complete positive dual nilradical. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-compatible-whittaker-characters** [claim](#stmt-lem-whittaker) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-whittaker): Use adjoint root coordinates for the Levi center and torus rescaling; use commutators and polynomial vanishing for nonsimple root groups. -> Split connected Levi center and a single torus orbit of nondegenerate unitary characters.
- **step-import-semisimple-parameters** [claim](#stmt-thm-parameters) / [proof](#pm-h-8): Apply the cited global excursion and local semisimple parameter theorems with the coefficient and occurrence interpretations supplied in the paper. -> Compatible local and global semisimple parameters without monodromy compatibility.
- **step-upgrade-tempered-completion** [claim](#stmt-thm-tempered-completion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-tempered-completion): Combine essential temperedness with unitary central character and boundedness in the adjoint and abelian quotients. -> A tempered completion in the full dual Levi.
- **step-identify-restriction-weil-part** [claim](#stmt-lem-wd-semisimplification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-wd-semisimplification): Use Jordan decomposition and cocharacter contraction to remove monodromy exponentials and unipotent Frobenius while preserving semisimplification. -> The Weil part equals the semisimplification of the restricted representation up to conjugacy.
- **step-cancel-monodromy-in-ratios** [claim](#stmt-lem-monodromy-ratio) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-monodromy-ratio): Compare inertia-invariant kernel determinants with the zero-monodromy determinants, using the dual quotient and Frobenius scaling. -> Functional-equation ratios differ by nonvanishing monomial units.
- **step-extract-purity-consequences** [claim](#stmt-lem-pure-determinant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pure-determinant): Use the lowest monodromy-chain degrees to control local-factor poles and symmetry of graded dimensions to compute the determinant. -> Half-plane holomorphy, nonvanishing, unimodular determinant, and inheritance by direct summands.
- **step-identify-open-monodromy-orbit** [claim](#stmt-prop-open-orbit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-open-orbit): Pair reciprocal Frobenius eigenspaces using the Killing form. Adjoint regularity annihilates the transpose kernel, making the orbit tangent map surjective. -> An open centralizer orbit and conjugacy of regular completions over the fixed Weil parameter.
- **step-trivialize-whittaker-families** [claim](#stmt-lem-whittaker-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-whittaker-family): Apply exact twisted coinvariants to the Bruhat filtration. The open cell supplies a free line and a Jacquet-functional generator surviving every specialization. -> A nonvanishing family trivialization identifying the inverse intertwining scalar with the local coefficient up to a unit.
- **step-factor-elementary-intertwiners** [claim](#stmt-lem-elementary-moves) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-elementary-moves): Choose a descending simple root, remove a maximal-parabolic right factor, and iterate with strictly decreasing Weyl length. -> Length-additive elementary factors and matching inversion partitions for roots and coroots.
- **step-globalize-supercuspidal-comparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-comparison) / [proof](#pm-h-18): Extend a finite-order central character, globalize generic supercuspidal data, and cancel the known principal-series factors between automorphic and curve functional equations. -> The maximal-parabolic coefficient comparison at the prescribed place.
- **step-complete-arbitrary-parabolic-comparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-elementary-moves-2): Use induction in stages and elementary factorization to multiply maximal-parabolic comparisons. Match the transported nilradicals by the coroot partition, then permit arbitrary monodromy. -> The total local-coefficient comparison for arbitrary generic inducing representations and standard parabolics.
- **step-prove-generic-adjoint-regularity** [claim](#stmt-prop-generic-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-generic-regularity): Use the generic image of the long intertwiner to obtain a nonzero Whittaker scalar. Apply the coefficient comparison and separate Levi and opposite-nilradical local factors. -> Adjoint regularity of the completion built from the Langlands datum.
- **step-contradict-positive-exponent** [claim](#stmt-thm-local-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-local-criterion): Assume non-temperedness. Conjugate the pure and inducing monodromy operators through their open orbit, transfer purity to the nilradical summand, and compare its pure and positively twisted determinants. -> Temperedness of the local representation.
- **step-import-companion-unramified-result** [claim](#stmt-thm-unramified) / [proof](#sec:global): Use the cited companion unramified Ramanujan theorem as a global input. -> Temperedness at all unramified places.
- **step-pass-global-purity-to-every-place** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-unramified): Extract local genericity from the global Whittaker functional. Unramified temperedness gives pointwise adjoint purity; local monodromy weights and semisimple compatibility supply a pure completion at every place. -> Tempered local components at every place, with the asserted unitary interpretation.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-monodromy-ratio-unit**: Changing the monodromy operator while retaining the same Weil representation changes the local functional-equation ratio only by a nonvanishing monomial factor. Individual local factors need not remain unchanged.
- **invariant-pure-determinant**: Weight-zero purity gives a Frobenius determinant of absolute value 1 and holomorphic, nonzero local factors in the stated right half-plane. These properties pass to Weil–Deligne direct summands.
- **invariant-regular-monodromy-orbit**: Adjoint regularity at the stated evaluation point forces an open centralizer orbit. Compatible monodromy operators satisfying this regularity over the same Weil parameter are conjugate under its centralizer.
- **invariant-whittaker-specialization**: The induced family's Whittaker coinvariants form a free rank-one module whose description commutes with specialization. Jacquet-integral generators remain nonzero at every twist, allowing the intertwining scalar to detect local-coefficient regularity at reducibility points.
- **invariant-pure-completion-temperedness**: An irreducible generic smooth representation is tempered when its semisimple local parameter has a Weil–Deligne completion with pure weight-zero adjoint representation. The proof compares regular monodromy orbits and derives a contradiction from the positive inducing exponent.
- **invariant-global-every-place**: Every local component of the stated globally generic cuspidal automorphic representation is tempered, including ramified components. The conclusion places no restriction on characteristic, ramification depth, or local representation type.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-semismple-monodromy-identification**: Identify the global monodromy operator with the operator obtained from tempered inducing data solely from semisimple local-global compatibility. -> The parameter theorem identifies the Weil part and leaves monodromy undetermined. Conjugacy requires the additional adjoint regularity argument.
- **anti-use-bounded-weil-shortcut**: Replace temperedness of a completion by boundedness of its associated Weil part. -> A tempered completion can have an unbounded Weil part because its associated Weil–Deligne representation retains the contribution of monodromy.
- **anti-use-exact-local-factor-equality**: Treat the local-coefficient comparison as exact equality of individual local factors for arbitrary monodromy. -> The comparison controls a product of ratios up to a nonzero constant times a monomial. Individual local factors can depend on monodromy.
- **anti-use-degenerate-specialization**: Infer local-coefficient regularity from an intertwining scalar computed using generators that may vanish after specialization. -> A vanishing generator can obscure the nonzero map on Whittaker coinvariants at a reducibility point.
- **anti-use-nongeneric-temperedness**: Apply the pure-completion criterion to an arbitrary irreducible smooth representation without checking genericity. -> The proof uses a generic Langlands quotient to obtain a nonzero intertwining map on Whittaker lines and hence adjoint regularity.
- **anti-use-nonadjoint-central-twist**: Transfer the local determinant contradiction unchanged to groups with real central twisting directions. -> The proof excludes a remaining real central twist using the adjoint hypothesis. The stated argument does not provide this exclusion for other groups.
- **anti-use-number-field-transfer**: Deduce the same global conclusion over number fields or at archimedean places. -> The global argument obtains purity from a lisse adjoint sheaf on an open curve over a finite field and its local monodromy weights.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper establishes temperedness for arbitrary smooth local representations and cuspidal automorphic representations through parameter existence, globalization, purity, and monodromy-orbit comparison. It supplies no finite input representation or terminating procedure for constructing these objects. The source states: "Let $G$ be split adjoint absolutely simple over a local function field $k$, and let $\tau$ be an irreducible generic smooth representation of $G(k)$. Suppose $\rho_\tau$ admits a Weil–Deligne completion $(\rho_\tau,N_*)$ whose adjoint representation is pure of weight zero. Then $\tau$ is tempered." Finite matrix examples of the auxiliary lemmas would check restricted instances rather than certify this representation-theoretic conclusion.

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |

| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The global temperedness theorem supplies a mathematical conclusion about automorphic representations, without an executable representation format or a software interface. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Purity controls Frobenius eigenvalues and local factors of Weil–Deligne representations; the paper supplies no game dynamics, physical evolution model, or simulation procedure. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The open-orbit criterion is a theoretical test under algebraic parameter hypotheses. A symbolic tool would additionally need finite encodings of the Weil parameter, inertia action, and centralizer that the paper does not specify. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The proof constructs elementary Weyl factors by choosing a simple root sent negative, adjoining it to the current Levi, and removing the resulting right factor. Strictly decreasing Weyl length supplies termination, while the inversion partition supplies a correctness check. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The free Whittaker coinvariant module is an algebraic object over a Laurent polynomial ring. Its freeness and specialization properties do not define a storage representation, update operation, or query contract. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The monodromy-ratio identity compares determinants and local factors as rational functions. It specifies no arithmetic workload distribution, hardware architecture, or acceleration guarantee. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The parameter theorem associates semisimple Weil and excursion parameters to representations and states compatibility properties. It provides no cryptographic primitive, adversary model, or hardness assumption. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Adjoint regularity is deduced from generic Langlands quotients with tempered inducing data. The paper gives no learning objective, statistical hypothesis, training rule, or prediction contract. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The positive chamber enters a contradiction proving temperedness, rather than an objective function or a feasible-set optimisation procedure. The criterion does not construct an optimal exponent. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Local-global compatibility and the passage from unramified to ramified places concern automorphic and Galois representations. Places are mathematical localizations, with no communication, replication, or failure-recovery contract. |
| other (Finite-dimensional representation theory) | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The paper explicitly constructs the monodromy filtration from Jordan chains. For a supplied finite-dimensional nilpotent operator with a Jordan-chain decomposition, this gives a concrete construction of the filtration used in the purity arguments. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-global-field-and-group-scope** (hypothesis): The global temperedness conclusion concerns globally generic complex cuspidal automorphic representations of the listed split connected adjoint exceptional groups over global function fields. Blocked use: ['Applying the global conclusion over number fields or at archimedean places.', 'Claiming the stated global theorem for arbitrary reductive groups.']
- **limit-genericity-and-cuspidality** (hypothesis): Global genericity and cuspidality are premises of the main theorem. The local criterion separately requires an irreducible generic smooth representation. Blocked use: ['Deducing temperedness for nongeneric cuspidal representations from the main theorem.', 'Applying the local criterion to an arbitrary smooth representation without checking irreducibility and genericity.']
- **limit-adjoint-central-twists** (hypothesis): The determinant contradiction uses the absence of real central twisting directions for an adjoint group. Extending it to groups with a nontrivial center requires additional central analysis. Blocked use: ['Transferring the local criterion unchanged to groups with real central twisting directions.']
- **limit-pure-completion-required** (hypothesis): The local criterion requires an actual Weil–Deligne completion whose adjoint representation is pure of weight zero. Existence of a semisimple local parameter alone does not supply this hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Concluding local temperedness merely from the existence of a semisimple Weil parameter.', 'Treating determinant absolute value alone as a substitute for adjoint purity.']
- **limit-semisimple-compatibility** (formal_scope): The parameter inputs identify semisimple Weil parameters and do not include compatibility of monodromy. Global and representation-theoretic completions must therefore remain separate until the orbit comparison applies. Blocked use: ['Identifying the global monodromy operator with the tempered inducing monodromy operator solely through local-global compatibility.']
- **limit-boundedness-versus-completion** (edge_case): The Weil part of a tempered completion need not be bounded. Purity concerns the completed representation and its monodromy filtration. Blocked use: ['Rejecting a tempered completion because its associated Weil part is unbounded.', 'Discarding monodromy before testing purity.']
- **limit-ratio-and-unit-scope** (formal_scope): The coefficient comparison controls a product of local-factor ratios up to a nonzero constant times a monomial. It does not assert exact equality of individually normalized local factors. Blocked use: ['Computing individual ramified local factors by setting monodromy to zero.', 'Recovering exact local-coefficient values or normalization constants from the divisor comparison.']
- **limit-finite-inertia-and-frobenius-semisimplicity** (hypothesis): The monodromy-ratio lemma assumes a finite-dimensional Frobenius-semisimple Weil representation with finite inertial image. The open-orbit proposition also requires finite inertia and semisimple Frobenius. Blocked use: ['Applying these statements to arbitrary continuous representations without establishing finite inertia and Frobenius semisimplicity.', 'Using the orbit conclusion outside the connected semisimple complex dual-group setting.']
- **limit-common-weil-part-and-regularity** (hypothesis): Conjugacy of monodromy operators requires the same Weil parameter and regularity of both adjoint local factors at the stated point. Blocked use: ['Conjugating completions with unrelated Weil parts.', 'Inferring conjugacy after establishing adjoint regularity for only one completion.']
- **limit-purity-summands-and-half-plane** (formal_scope): The purity conclusions pass to Weil–Deligne direct summands. The asserted holomorphy and nonvanishing region is the open right half-plane. Blocked use: ['Passing purity to an arbitrary subspace without proving it is a Weil–Deligne direct summand.', 'Extending the holomorphy assertion to the boundary or beyond the stated half-plane.']
- **limit-whittaker-specialization** (hypothesis): At reducibility points, interpreting the intertwining scalar requires generators of the Whittaker lines that remain nonzero under specialization. Blocked use: ['Inferring local-coefficient regularity from a scalar computed using a generator that vanishes at the specialization.', 'Applying the free-line argument without generic inducing data.']
- **limit-positive-exponent-contradiction** (edge_case): The strict determinant inequality requires a proper parabolic and strictly positive central-weight evaluations. The strict inequality is not supplied at a chamber boundary. Blocked use: ['Using the strict determinant contradiction with a nonproper parabolic.', 'Retaining the strict inequality when the positive-weight hypothesis is absent.']
- **limit-elementary-factorization-input** (hypothesis): The elementary Weyl factorization requires a standard Levi and the stated condition on the images of its simple roots. Termination is proved through strict decrease of residual Weyl length. Blocked use: ['Running the elementary factorization on a Weyl element without checking the simple-root image condition.', 'Replacing maximal-parabolic elementary moves by torus-induced rank-one calculations without establishing the additional torus hypothesis.']
- **limit-filtration-does-not-certify-purity** (formal_scope): Constructing the monodromy filtration from Jordan chains does not itself establish purity. Purity additionally prescribes Frobenius eigenvalue absolute values on every graded piece with respect to the fixed complex realization. Blocked use: ['Declaring purity solely because a nilpotent operator admits Jordan chains.', 'Ignoring the coefficient realization when checking the purity hypothesis.']
- **limit-globalization-and-parameter-existence** (nonconstructive): The coefficient comparison uses existence of a globally generic cuspidal globalization with a prescribed local component and finite-order central character. The global argument also chooses an occurring excursion parameter; neither passage supplies a finite computational representation of these objects. Blocked use: ['Presenting the globalization argument as an executable constructor for automorphic vectors.', 'Treating the existence of an occurring excursion parameter as an algorithm for computing it.']
- **limit-geometric-purity-input** (hypothesis): The passage from unramified temperedness to ramified temperedness uses pointwise purity of a lisse adjoint sheaf on an open curve and the local monodromy weight theorem in the fixed coefficient realization. Blocked use: ['Inferring ramified temperedness from unramified temperedness without supplying the global parameter and geometric purity inputs.', 'Transferring the argument to another global setting solely by analogy.']
- **limit-no-general-executable-temperedness-test** (nonconstructive): The main conclusion is obtained through parameter existence, globalization, geometric purity, and a contradiction using Langlands classification. Finite constructive auxiliary arguments do not constitute a decision procedure for arbitrary smooth or automorphic representations. Blocked use: ['Advertising an executable general temperedness test based on finite matrix examples.', 'Treating verification of auxiliary root or filtration constructions as certification of the global theorem.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the generalized Ramanujan conjecture for globally generic cuspidal automorphic representations of split connected adjoint exceptional groups over global function fields. Every local component is tempered, without restrictions on characteristic or ramification depth. The proof extends the unramified Ramanujan theorem of a companion paper to all ramified places.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

Let $X$ be a smooth projective geometrically connected curve over $\mathbb F_q$, let $F=\mathbb F_q(X)$, and let $G/F$ be a split connected adjoint absolutely simple group. Fix a split Borel subgroup $B=TN$, and write $\mathbb A_F$ for the adeles of $F$. A cuspidal automorphic representation $\pi$ of $G(\mathbb A_F)$ is *globally generic* if some vector has a nonzero Whittaker coefficient $$\int_{N(F)\backslash N(\mathbb A_F)} f(ng)\psi(n)^{-1}\,dn$$ for a character $\psi$ nontrivial in every simple-root coordinate. The generalized Ramanujan conjecture predicts tempered local components for such representations. Here temperedness means that the unitary local representation is weakly contained in the regular representation. This is the generic temperedness prediction within the broader framework of Arthur parameters <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Arthur1989](#ref-Arthur1989), [Shahidi2011](#ref-Shahidi2011)].

We prove the exceptional-group case, including the places where the representation is ramified. The global input is the unramified Ramanujan theorem of the companion paper <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[UnramifiedRamanujan](#ref-UnramifiedRamanujan), Corollary 1.2].

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $G/F$ be split connected adjoint absolutely simple of type $G_2$, $F_4$, $E_6$, $E_7$, or $E_8$. If $\pi=\bigotimes'_v\pi_v$ is a complex globally generic cuspidal automorphic representation of $G(\mathbb A_F)$, then $\pi_v$ is tempered for every place $v$. There is no restriction on the characteristic of $F$, the ramification depth, or the local representation type.*

</div>

The distinction between spherical and ramified components is substantial. An unramified parameter is determined by its Satake class, whereas at a ramified place a Weil–Deligne parameter also contains a nilpotent monodromy operator. The local-global theorem available for general reductive groups identifies only the semisimple Weil parameter. The argument below supplies the additional monodromy comparison needed for temperedness.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and the local problem

Over function fields, Drinfeld proved Ramanujan for $\mathrm{GL}_2$ <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Dri88](#ref-Dri88)], and Laurent Lafforgue proved it for $\mathrm{GL}_n$ <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Laf02](#ref-Laf02), Theoreme VI.10(i)]. For other reductive groups, genericity selects the part of the cuspidal spectrum for which temperedness is expected. The Langlands–Shahidi method relates this condition to local coefficients and automorphic $L$-functions. Lomelí developed this method over function fields and proved generic Ramanujan for split classical groups and quasi-split unitary groups <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Lom09](#ref-Lom09), [LomeliLS](#ref-LomeliLS), [Lom19](#ref-Lom19)]. In particular, the later split classical results include characteristic $2$ <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Lom19](#ref-Lom19), Theorem 7.1(i)].

Vincent Lafforgue’s excursion operators attach semisimple global parameters to cuspidal automorphic representations of general reductive groups <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[VLafforgue](#ref-VLafforgue)]. Genestier–Lafforgue construct semisimple local parameters and prove local-global compatibility at every place <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[GenestierLafforgue](#ref-GenestierLafforgue)]. Gan–Harris–Sawin, with an appendix by Beuzart-Plessis, show that the local parameter of a tempered representation admits an essentially tempered completion <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[GHS](#ref-GHS), Theorem 1.2 and Corollary 1.3]. These results make it possible to compare the global and representation-theoretic sources of monodromy without assuming a full local correspondence.

On the unramified side, Sawin–Templier prove temperedness under a monomial geometric supercuspidal hypothesis and a cyclic base-change hypothesis <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[SawinTemplier2021](#ref-SawinTemplier2021)]. Ciubotaru–Harris obtain temperedness at unramified places under hypotheses including a generic unramified place and a tempered place <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[CH26](#ref-CH26)]. The companion result used here gives the unramified conclusion for split adjoint absolutely simple groups from a generic unramified component alone <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[UnramifiedRamanujan](#ref-UnramifiedRamanujan), Corollary 1.2]. The present paper proves the local implication that extends this conclusion to the places of level.

Two established principles guide that implication. First, genericity is related to regularity at $1$ of the adjoint $L$-factor, as formulated in the Gross–Prasad/Rallis conjecture <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[GrossPrasad1992](#ref-GrossPrasad1992), Conjecture 2.6]; see, for example, the criterion proved by Gan–Ichino under local-correspondence and local-factor hypotheses <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[GanIchino2016](#ref-GanIchino2016), Appendix B, Proposition B.1]. Second, adjoint regularity is equivalent to openness of the monodromy orbit over a fixed semisimple parameter <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[CDFZ2025](#ref-CDFZ2025), Proposition 3.5] and <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[DHKM2026](#ref-DHKM2026), Proposition 6.10]. We give the short Lie-algebra proof of the implication needed here. The broader principle that purity strongly constrains a completion of a semisimple Weil representation also appears in Taylor–Yoshida <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[TaylorYoshida2007](#ref-TaylorYoshida2007), Section 1].

Our task is to obtain the required adjoint regularity for arbitrary generic inducing data using the semisimple correspondence alone. We compare the *total* local coefficient with a product of Weil–Deligne $L$-factor ratios. Multiplicativity, rank-one Tate factors, the globalization theorem of Gan–Lomelí, and the crude functional equation of Lomelí provide the comparison <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[GanLomeli](#ref-GanLomeli), [LomeliLS](#ref-LomeliLS)]. An elementary cancellation shows that these ratios do not depend on monodromy up to nonvanishing monomials. This is exactly the amount of local-factor compatibility required by the argument. Genestier–Lafforgue also construct local $\gamma$-factors from semisimple parameters and characterize them through global functional equations <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[GenestierLafforgue](#ref-GenestierLafforgue), Section 7].

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The proof and its reusable local criterion

The proof has a local part and a global source of purity. At a fixed place, let $r$ be the semisimple Weil parameter of a generic local representation. A Weil–Deligne completion is a nilpotent operator $N$ satisfying $\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(r(w))N=|w|N$. The local result is Theorem <a href="#thm:local-criterion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:local-criterion">13</a>: if a completion has adjoint representation pure of weight zero, then the representation is tempered. Purity here prescribes Frobenius eigenvalue sizes along monodromy chains, with weights symmetric about zero. This criterion applies to every split adjoint absolutely simple group over a local function field.

To see the mechanism, suppose the representation is not tempered. Its Langlands datum consists of a tempered representation $\sigma$ of a proper Levi subgroup and a strictly positive real exponent $\nu$. A tempered completion for $\sigma$, twisted by $\nu$, gives a completion $(r,N_M)$. The long intertwiner has generic image, so its scalar on Whittaker coinvariants is nonzero. The local-coefficient comparison then makes the adjoint $L$-factor of $(r,N_M)$ regular at $1$. A completion $(r,N_*)$ with pure adjoint has the same regularity. Both operators therefore lie in the unique open orbit of the centralizer of $r$, and are conjugate. Figure <a href="#fig:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:comparison">1</a> displays this comparison.

<figure id="fig:comparison">

<figcaption>The local comparison uses two independently obtained monodromy operators. Adjoint regularity identifies their orbits over the fixed Weil parameter $r$.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-comparison"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:comparison`)

  {Completion $(r,N_*)$ with pure adjoint\\

  {Generic quotient of\\positive Langlands data\\

 \node[box,text width=8.7cm] (orbit) at (3.45,-2.1)
